# 3D Reconstruction Daily Papers

The project automatically fetches the latest arXiv papers for 3D reconstruction and 3D Gaussian Splatting keywords.

The subheadings represent the search keywords. For each keyword, the table keeps the most recently updated arXiv records returned by the API.

Click the repository's Watch button to receive notifications from the daily issue workflow.

Last update: 2026-11-14

## 3D reconstruction
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |

| **[BrickFlow: Connectivity-Guided Brick Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17321v1)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Given a set of LEGO parts and an image of an assembled object, our task is to infer the pose of each part to assemble the object. In contrast with traditional 3D reconstruction, doing so requires satisfying discrete physical constraints, which provide structure but render the problem combinatorial and highly sensitive to small pose errors. To learn a prior over how parts fit together, we train a set-conditioned SE(3) flow model on procedurally generated assemblies. We then finetune this model to inject image conditioning, using 1% as much data. However, while the learned flow captures global structure, it does not enforce connectivity. To address this, we introduce an analytic connectivity-field guidance to pull predictions toward connected assemblies at test time. Through this, we demonstrate improved build quality with model size, pretraining, and guidance. We make our models and data available for research purposes at https://kulits.github.io/BrickFlow/</p></details> | NeurIPS 2026 |
| **[GC-MoE on SM120: Overlapping Expert-Parallel Token Dispatch with Warp-Level FP8xFP4 Block-Scaled MMA on Consumer Blackwell](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16219v1)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Expert-parallel Mixture-of-Experts decoding is latency bound: each layer serializes a token exchange, a grouped GEMM over routed experts, and a combine, while only a few tokens per rank are in flight. Prior GC-MoE fused these phases into one persistent, Green-Context-partitioned CUDA Graph on Hopper (SM90) and datacenter Blackwell (SM100). This paper rebuilds the operator for SM120 (compute capability 12.0, e.g. RTX PRO 5000) over PCIe. SM120 exposes block-scaled tensor cores only through warp-level mma.sync.aligned.kind::mxf8f6f4: no tensor memory, no two-CTA MMA, no cluster multicast. We rebuild the kernel around a swap-AB warp-level block-scaled MMA with FP4 weights in the register A operand and FP8 activations in B, plus SM120-specific mechanisms: scale-byte selection through MMA immediates, a compact-weights path, an adaptive token-tile geometry, an expert-granular streaming dispatch with a batched bulk-pull engine and a 64-bit deduplicating pull cache, full-width BF16 returns drained by resident relay copiers over a position-major arena, and shared experts folded into the compute role. On 8xRTX PRO 5000 over PCIe the operator is 1.522x and 1.387x faster than the FlashInfer CuTe DSL W4A8 MegaMoE and the Cake MegaMoE across 18 decode points (18/18 wins each), and 1.388x faster than Cake with shared experts fused; all 94 numerical cases and 752 per-rank checks pass, at cross-operator cosine similarity of at least 0.9999997.</p></details> | 24 pages, 10 tables, 6 figures |
| **[LILA: Data-Free Structured Pruning of LLMs via Latent Spectral Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11163v2)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Structured pruning of large language models (LLMs) has universally required calibration data, gradient computation, or large auxiliary policy networks at pruning time. LILA (\emph{Latent-Informed Layer Analysis}) demonstrates that this dependency is avoidable: each FFN weight matrix is factorized with Non-negative Matrix Factorization (NMF) to obtain a parts-based spectral representation, and neuron importance is scored via the Kolmogorov--Smirnov (KS) distance between singular value distributions of the full and neuron-ablated NMF matrix, a closed-form criterion requiring no calibration corpus, forward pass, or gradient computation. Two data-free matrix variants (Abs, Split) generate pruning masks from weight spectral geometry alone; a calibration-guided variant (Act) is provided as an internal ablation. Evaluation spans 7 model families (up to 70B). Without fine-tuning, data-free LILA surpasses PruneNet (45M-parameter RL policy) by up to 2.12~pp in zero-shot accuracy on LLaMA-2-7B and outperforms WikiText-2-calibrated SliceGPT by up to 6.1~pp across all sparsity levels, confirming data-free performance is not an artifact of omitting activation statistics. After one LoRA RFT epoch (matched WikiText-2 protocol), LILA Act surpasses SliceGPT+RFT by 6.39~pp on average using 98.4\% fewer calibration samples (16 vs.\ 1,024) across LLaMA-2-7B and Phi-2. NTK analysis confirms a $17{\times}$ reduction in functional distortion versus random pruning, providing theoretical grounding for the spectral criterion.</p></details> | 31 Pages |
| **[SpaceMCP: Vision Language Model Spatial Reasoning via Model Context Protocol](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15680v1)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Despite impressive advances in large language models (LLM), vision-language models (VLM) still exhibit limited spatial reasoning capabilities. Recent progress in feed-forward 3D reconstruction has enabled precise, real-time geometric understanding from visual inputs. In this work, we propose SpaceMCP, a framework that leverages these advances to introduce an MCP-style paradigm for spatial reasoning. Our approach augments a VLM with structured function calls, allowing it to query a scene graph for spatial information such as distances, object positioning, and camera properties. The scene graph is constructed in real time using feed-forward 3D reconstruction to recover metric geometry, combined with open-vocabulary segmentation to identify and localize relevant objects within the scene. Paired with the VLM's strong reasoning capabilities, the VLM is able to combine this primitive information to solve more complex spatial tasks. Without any training, SpaceMCP achieves state-of-the-art results on VSI-Bench, MMSI-Bench and VSTI-Bench, outperforming recent spatially post-trained VLMs and improving its base VLM by 15-22 points per benchmark. We argue that, given the contrast between the relatively slow progress in native VLM spatial reasoning and the rapid advances in feed-forward 3D computer vision, this paradigm offers a promising path forward for enabling robust spatial reasoning.</p></details> | 13 pages, 3 figures. Accepted at the 2nd Workshop on Embodied Spatial Reasoning (ESR) at NeurIPS 2026 |
| **[ThermalSplat: Generalizable Pose-Free Gaussian Splatting for Sparse-View Thermal 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15435v1)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Thermal infrared imaging is vital for applications like search-and-rescue and autonomous navigation, yet trans- lating it into 3D scene representations remains a critical bottleneck. The low-texture nature and view-dependent emissivity of thermal images cause standard pose estima- tors to fail catastrophically, collapsing standard 3D Gaus- sian Splatting pipelines under sparse-view constraints. Fur- thermore, existing thermal reconstruction methods rely heavily on dense capture and computationally expensive per-scene optimization. Critically, many of these ap- proaches depend on aligned RGB-thermal datasets to ob- tain reliable camera poses and initial point clouds, ren- dering them unusable in purely thermal, unposed envi- ronments. To break this bottleneck, we present Thermal- Splat, the first generalizable, feed-forward Gaussian splat- ting framework that reconstructs 3D scenes directly from sparse thermal images without requiring known camera poses, RGB guidance, or per-scene training. We over- come thermal-specific challenges using a soft census loss for brightness-invariant structural matching and a bound- ary structure loss to explicitly supervise temperature discon- tinuities. Trained on an aggregated multi-source dataset, ThermalSplat synthesizes sharp reconstructions of unseen scenes in a single feed-forward pass. Extensive experi- ments demonstrate that ThermalSplat holds a strict advan- tage over scene-specific thermal splatting methods in the sparse regime. Furthermore, even compared against these baselines operating under their ideal dense capture set- tings, ThermalSplat achieves superior perceptual fidelity. By eliminating the need for computationally expensive per scene optimization, ThermalSplat offers an efficient and generalizable approach to thermal 3D reconstruction.</p></details> | WACV 2027 |
| **[GUSH3R: Everyone Everywhere All at Once as Gaussians](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05243v2)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Reconstructing dynamic human-scene environments from monocular videos is a challenging problem that requires jointly modeling scene geometry, camera motion, and non-rigid human dynamics while enabling photorealistic rendering. Recent feed-forward methods can efficiently predict geometry, but they are often limited to non-photorealistic representations such as point clouds and meshes, or they fail to handle non-rigid objects, particularly dynamic humans. To fill this gap, we present GUSH3R (Gaussian-Unified Scene Human 3D Reconstruction), a feed-forward framework for online dynamic human-scene reconstruction. From a monocular human-scene video, our method reconstructs dynamic humans (everyone) and static scenes (everywhere) in a single forward pass (all at once) as 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) primitives (as gaussians), which are geometrically consistent and capable of novel view synthesis. Experiments on monocular human-scene datasets demonstrate that our approach achieves competitive novel view synthesis quality while significantly improving inference efficiency compared to optimization-based methods.</p></details> | Project page: https://abkeito.github.io/gush3r-page/ Accepted to WACV 2027 |
| **[A Multi-Parent Edge Assembly Crossover Driven Hybrid Genetic Algorithm for the Hamiltonian \textit{p}-Median Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15219v1)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The Hamiltonian \textit{p}-median problem seeks to partition the vertices of a graph into exactly $p$ disjoint cycles while minimizing their total cost. This paper presents a hybrid genetic algorithm incorporating a multi-parent edge assembly crossover operator. The crossover combines a base solution with multiple donor solutions in a single recombination operation, thereby expanding the space of available edge exchanges. It uses virtual copies of the base solution to establish edge balance, together with an auxiliary directed graph and a budgeted search to construct valid AB-cycles. Extensive experiments on 215 benchmark instances across four sets show that the proposed algorithm improves the best-known solution values for 34 instances and matches those for another 168. Additional experiments indicate that a moderate number of parents provides beneficial diversity, whereas excessive parent counts, particularly when $p$ is large, lead to excessive population dispersion and hinder convergence.</p></details> |  |
| **[MatrixSplat: Matrix-Parallel and Feature-Scalable 3D Gaussian Splatting Rendering on GPUs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15153v1)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has emerged as a mainstream representation for real-time novel view synthesis and 3D reconstruction. However, existing 3DGS rendering pipelines are poorly aligned with modern GPU architectures: depth-sorted alpha compositing introduces strong per-pixel sequential dependencies, limiting parallelism and resulting in scalar FMA-dominated execution that underutilizes high-throughput matrix units such as Tensor Cores. This mismatch becomes more severe for multimodal and semantically enriched 3DGS, where high-dimensional features must be accumulated from millions of Gaussians, leading to rapidly increasing computation and memory costs. We propose MatrixSplat, a matrix-parallel rendering framework for feature-scalable 3DGS on modern GPUs. We first identify the sequential dependency of alpha compositing as a key obstacle to efficient high-dimensional feature rendering, and adopt an order-independent weighted accumulation formulation that transforms feature rendering into matrix-oriented computation. Our framework features three key designs: (i) a tile-based splatting pipeline that maps feature accumulation to warp-level matrix-multiply-accumulate execution on Tensor Cores; (ii) a warp-granularity Gaussian packing strategy that mitigates visibility irregularity and improves feature reuse; and (iii) a lazy channel-batched feature-fetching pipeline that reduces shared-memory pressure, maintains high warp occupancy, and overlaps memory access with matrix execution for high-dimensional features. On Mip-NeRF 360, LERF, and 3D-OVS, MatrixSplat achieves up to 3.8$\times$ speedup over 3DGS and 2$\times$ over TC-GS while maintaining rendering quality and semantic accuracy, and further reduces render-kernel time by up to 36.7$\times$ for high-dimensional features. Integrated into existing fast renderers, it provides an additional 1.69-2.84$\times$ speedup.</p></details> | Accepted to NeurIPS 2026 |
| **[RIGOR: Rig-Informed Geometry for Omnidirectional Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.13504v3)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Recent developments in feed-forward 3D reconstruction resulted in models which can recover dense scene representations and camera motion solely from an image stream. However, such predictions are prone to becoming inconsistent over long trajectories, specifically in demanding environments with repetitive structures, weak textures and dynamic objects or people. One way to mitigate those challenges is to use an omnidirectional camera, which provides wide spatial coverage and captures richer visual information. Yet, the majority of models do not offer support for 360-degree imagery or require additional fine-tuning. To bridge these two aspects, we present RIGOR: a large-scale reconstruction pipeline for gravity-aligned omnidirectional videos that retains a frozen feed-forward perspective backbone and exploits each panorama as a four-view virtual rig. The rig structure is used to detect and repair locally inconsistent predictions, to retrieve loop closures through cyclic four-view consensus, and to geometrically verify candidate revisits before global optimization. Verified constraints drive a Sim(3) pose graph that corrects accumulated rotation, translation, and scale drift along the sequence. We demonstrate that the proposed consistency mechanisms improve both trajectory accuracy and reconstructed geometry over a feed-forward baseline on challenging construction-site sequences. The code is made available under this link: https://github.com/TangentH/RIGOR.</p></details> |  |
| **[Multi-View 3D Reconstruction Using V-JEPA Features](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15143v1)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Predictive video encoders provide dense visual representations, but adapting them to 3D reconstruction requires accurate depth, camera poses, and cross-view geometry. We study supervised RGB-only multi-view reconstruction using an architecture that combines video-pretrained features, camera estimation, scene aggregation, epipolar transport, and residual pointmaps. Experiments compare global and spatial camera regression with joint relative objectives on CO3D, alongside an independently trained TUM RGB-D model. On 168 CO3D five-view windows, the spatial-camera configuration achieves 29.3% lower absolute trajectory error than the global-camera configuration, but performs worse than identity poses in trajectory error and worse than the global configuration in depth error. The joint relative-objective configuration improves normalized intrinsics error and similarity-aligned Chamfer distance from 0.6447 to 0.5573, but increases raw Chamfer distance from 3.5535 to 4.4374. On 163 indoor windows, camera error is 0.3063 m versus 0.3694 m for identity poses, while depth error is 0.4279 versus 0.2516 for constant median depth. Refinement worsens indoor camera error, and additional experiments show competing depth and pose effects. Overall, the results distinguish representation adaptation, aligned shape, and raw reconstruction, highlighting the need for isolated component and encoder comparisons before attributing reconstruction gains to predictive video pretraining.</p></details> | 29 pages, 11 figures. Source code is available at https://github.com/Anwarshamim01/multi-view-3d-reconstruction-using-v-jepa-features |
| **[Unified Panoramic Geometry Estimation via Multi-View Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26368v3)** | 2026-11-10 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Geometry estimation from perspective images has greatly advanced, maturing to the point where off-the-shelf foundation models are able to reconstruct 3D scene structure not only from multi-view imagery, but even from a single view. A natural extension is 3D reconstruction from panoramas, with the exciting prospect of recovering a full 360-degree scene from a single panoramic image. In this work, we introduce PaGeR (Panoramic Geometry Reconstruction), a framework to lift powerful 3D foundation models designed for perspective imagery to the panorama domain. Our strategy is to start from a pre-trained transformer for 3D reconstruction and turn it into a unified high-performance model that predicts scale-invariant depth, metric depth, surface normals, and sky masks from both perspective and omnidirectional images, in a single forward pass. By keeping architectural changes to a minimum and mixing perspective and panoramic images during training, PaGeR retains the rich 3D prior of the underlying foundation model while learning to also estimate geometrically consistent 360-degree scenes from single panoramas. We extensively test our method in both indoor and outdoor environments and find that it delivers state-of-the-art performance and excellent zero-shot performance across a wide range of scenes. Code, data and models are available $\href{https://github.com/prs-eth/PaGeR}{\text{here}}$.</p></details> |  |
| **[Pygmalion Effect in Vision: Image-to-Clay Translation for Reflective Geometry Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.21098v2)** | 2026-11-10 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Understanding reflection remains a long-standing challenge in 3D reconstruction due to the entanglement of appearance and geometry under view-dependent reflections. In this work, we present the Pygmalion Effect in Vision, a novel framework that metaphorically "sculpts" reflective objects into clay-like forms through image-to-clay translation. We then introduce a dual-branch design in which a BRDF-based reflective branch and a clay-guided branch share the same Gaussian geometry but operate through independent rendering paths. The clay-guided branch, supervised by the synthesized clay images, provides reflection-free geometric guidance that complements the photometric supervision of the BRDF branch. Experiments on both synthetic and real datasets show consistent improvements in both geometry and photometric metrics. Beyond technical gains, our framework reveals that seeing by unshining, translating radiance into neutrality, can serve as a powerful inductive bias for reflective object geometry learning.</p></details> | Accepted at NeurIPS 2026 |
| **[An exact compress-with-another threshold in a binary automaton family](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07044v2)** | 2026-11-10 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>For a state $q$ in a synchronizing automaton, let $μ(q)$ be the minimum length of a word merging $q$ with another initial state. Szykuła asked whether $μ(q)=O(n)$ for strongly connected synchronizing automata and described a binary family attributed to Dżyga, with $n=3p$ states and a conjectured threshold $4n/3$. We specify the boundary transitions omitted from the published piecewise definition and prove that the resulting automata $\mathcal A_p$ satisfy $μ(q_0)=4p=4n/3$ for all $p\geq3$. A shortest word merging $q_0$ with another state is $(ba)^p(ab)^p$. We also construct a reset word of length $3p^2+4p-1$. Consequently, any uniform linear upper bound for $μ(q)$ on binary strongly connected synchronizing automata with corank-one letters has leading constant at least $4/3$.</p></details> | 9 pages, 1 figure. Revised proofs and exposition, corrected boundary transitions, updated references, and added a coauthor |
| **[Lightweight Deep Learning Framework for Astrocyte-Centric 3D Reconstruction across Normal and Stress-associated Ultrastructural States](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13581v1)** | 2026-11-10 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The morphology of astrocytes is closely associated with their ability to support and modulate neural circuit functions; however, its reconstruction from volume electron microscopy (vEM) data remains labor-intensive and computationally resource-intensive, placing substantial barriers on its broader adoption and scalability despite the high spatial resolution of vEM. We present AstroSeg-vEM, a lightweight, astrocyte-centric framework for morphology-preserving astrocyte segmentation, 3D reconstruction, and quantitative morphological analysis in vEM images. AstroSeg-vEM combines transformer-based 2D segmentation with dedicated post-processing methods and operates directly on high-resolution EM sections, making it well suited to anisotropic vEM data and downstream proofreading workflows while preserving the full in-plane resolution of the original EM data. Across four mouse hippocampal datasets representing normal and morphologically defined stress-associated astrocyte ultrastructural states, AstroSeg-vEM demonstrates robust performance and generalization, achieving average Dice and weighted Dice values of 0.92 and 0.95, respectively, on a held-out test dataset. Its lightweight architecture enables efficient, high-fidelity reconstruction on consumer-grade hardware, providing a scalable and accessible platform for large-scale astrocyte ultrastructure analysis and future astrocyte-neuron interaction studies.</p></details> | Accepted for publication in npj Imaging. 19 pages, 4 figures, 6 tables, including Supplementary Material. Code is available at https://github.com/QuIN-Lab/AstroSeg-vEM |
| **[Camera3R: Feed-Forward 3D Reconstruction from Heterogeneous Camera Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13453v1)** | 2026-11-10 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Feed-forward 3D reconstruction (3R) from unconstrained multi-view images has recently emerged as a prominent research direction in 3D computer vision. Various foundational reconstruction models have been proposed with different architectures and reconstruction paradigms. Despite being trained on large-scale perspective datasets with substantial computational resources, these models generally struggle to generalize to non-perspective images. This limitation hinders their deployment in applications involving arbitrary, unknown, and potentially heterogeneous camera models. To build on the capabilities of existing reconstruction models with minimal retraining, we propose Camera3R, a low-rank adaptation framework that efficiently extends perspective-pretrained 3R networks to any other camera models. Extensive experiments demonstrate that, with only 0.5% additional parameters and less than a day of training, Camera3R adapts a foundational 3D reconstruction model to non-perspective cameras while fully preserving its original performance on perspective inputs. Code is available at https://github.com/android-xr/camera3r.</p></details> | 40th Conference on Neural Information Processing Systems (NeurIPS 2026) |
| **[Incorporating World Models in Decision Making for Next Best View Selection in Active Sensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13229v1)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Efficient robotic 3D reconstruction requires selecting views that contribute new surface measurements with few physical captures. Pretrained world models can guide this choice, but a predicted surface may not produce valid depth measurements under the operating conditions of the deployed sensor. We present a deployment adaptation methodology for next-best-view selection that learns a sensor-return adapter from limited local calibration data while keeping the pretrained predictor fixed. The adapter estimates measurement dropout over each predicted surface, allowing the selection method to favor views likely to fill gaps in the measured reconstruction. In a real-robot experiment with a structured-light sensor, the adapted method achieves higher measured surface coverage with 60% fewer captures than nominal selection. The adapted method recovers a surface region that the nominal method repeatedly targets but fails to measure. These results suggest that robots can reuse pretrained predictive models while adapting their decisions to local operating conditions in data-scarce environments.</p></details> | Accepted to CoRL 2026 Workshop "Do Robots Need World Models?" |
| **[Click3R: Interactive Stereo 3D Reconstruction with Sparse Correspondence Clicks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12875v1)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Despite impressive progress, existing feed-forward large 3D reconstruction models produce a single, irrevocable prediction. When these models fail, there is no mechanism to correct their mistakes. To address this limitation, we introduce Click3R, a framework for interactive geometric correction of feed-forward models using sparse human correspondence clicks. Given an image pair and a small set of cross-view point correspondences provided by a user, Click3R injects geometric constraints into the 3D reconstruction networks through a lightweight point correspondence adapter, enabling the model to resolve ambiguities in challenging scenarios such as repetitive textures, symmetric structures, large viewpoint changes, or textureless regions. To evaluate interactive reconstruction, we curate a challenging benchmark specifically designed to expose failure modes of existing feed-forward 3D reconstruction methods. Experiments show that Click3R reduces reconstruction error with as few as a single correspondence click, improving performance on both standard and our curated benchmarks. Our results highlight that sparse human interaction provides an effective and practical mechanism for correcting geometric reconstruction errors.</p></details> | NeurIPS 2026. Project page: https://uva-computer-vision-lab.github.io/click3r/ |
| **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset comprises 770 RANS simulations of 194 unique wing configurations, sampled across 27 airfoil profile variants and 4 distances from the ground, spanning variations in wing sweep, chord, taper, angle of attack, and tip twist. Six neural network architectures representing distinct paradigms --- convolutional (DoMINO), graph-based (X-MeshGraphNet), transformer-based (AB-UPT, GeoTransolver, GAOT), and JEPA-based (AeroJEPA) --- are evaluated on their ability to predict surface pressure coefficient and wall shear stress distributions, from which integral drag and lift coefficients are recovered. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |
| **[HIST3R: Decomposing and Rectifying State Updates with History for Streaming 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12011v1)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Streaming 3D reconstruction with recurrent states enables long-video scene understanding under bounded memory, but turns reconstruction into a state-evolution problem: each incoming frame updates a compact scene memory on which all subsequent predictions depend, so unreliable updates overwrite historical context and accumulate into long-horizon geometric drift. We analyze CUT3R-style recurrent updates through a direction--magnitude decomposition, in which the magnitude sets the update gain, i.e., how strongly an observation is written into the state, and the direction encodes the geometric intent, i.e., where the update drives the state. This decomposition provides a unified view of CUT3R and its variants, revealing that existing training-free extensions perform magnitude-only control: they can attenuate an unreliable update but cannot correct its direction. We propose HIST3R, a training-free framework built on CUT3R that controls both components using history. HIST3R rectifies the update direction using locally consistent historical states, and modulates the update magnitude according to the visual discrepancy between the current observation and its history, suppressing redundant updates while preserving complementary evidence. Across camera pose estimation, 3D reconstruction, and video depth estimation, HIST3R consistently improves long-horizon performance while retaining the memory footprint and inference speed of CUT3R. With 1000 input frames, it reduces ATE by 23.87% on ScanNet and 33.65% on TUM-Dynamics relative to the strongest competing method; with 500 frames, it improves median reconstruction accuracy over the strongest CUT3R-based variant by 56.52% on 7-Scenes and 33.96% on NRGBD.</p></details> | Accepted at NeurIPS 2026. Project page: https://kennethyangg.github.io/HIST3R/ |
| **[3R-Adapter: Retrieval, Rewiring, and Refinement for Efficient Adaptation of 3D Reconstruction Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11964v1)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Feed-forward 3D reconstruction models have demonstrated strong generalization under large-scale pre-training, yet they struggle in long-tail scenarios such as low-light reconstruction, joint human-scene reconstruction, and test-time adaptation. We observe relational attention structure collapse, manifested as the failure of cross-view correspondences, as a primary cause of these limitations. While adaptation offers a practical way to bridge this gap, existing parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) approaches mainly perform channel-wise feature adjustment and lack mechanisms to explicitly adapt relational structures, leaving correspondence failures unresolved. In this work, we formulate adaptation of 3D reconstruction models as a problem of correspondence recovery under domain shift and propose 3R-Adapter, a PEFT paradigm for long-tail 3D reconstruction scenarios. Our method consists of three components: Deformable Retrieval for task-adaptive relation candidate retrieval, Relational Rewiring for reconstructing cross-view relational structures, and Consistency Refinement for injecting the rewired relations into stable predictions. Extensive experiments on low-light reconstruction, joint human-scene reconstruction, and test-time adaptation demonstrate that our approach significantly improves robustness over existing PEFT methods while maintaining efficient lightweight adaptation. Our code is released at https://github.com/PKU-OV3-LAB/3R-Adapter.</p></details> | Accepted as NeurIPS 2026 Spotlight |
| **[Budget-aware Correspondence-driven View Selection for Real-time 3D Perception of Mobile Robots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11809v1)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>3D visual perception and scene reconstruction are critical for autonomous robots and intelligent transportation, yet real-world deployments often generate long, redundant image sequences that hinder downstream processing. This report investigates BCVS (budget-aware correspondence-driven view sampling), a pose-free view selection algorithm that offers 22+ FPS on a CPU, enabling online filtering for efficient 3D perception by mobile robots. Unlike existing approaches that rely on full structure-from-motion (SfM) pipelines or camera pose estimation, BCVS employs a purely correspondence-based scoring strategy that prioritizes view novelty while maintaining sufficient overlap for geometric stability. Given a user-specified budget, BCVS iteratively selects the most distinct frames and suppresses near-duplicates, producing compact subsets that preserve connectivity and minimize reconstruction loss. Extensive experiments on benchmark show that BCVS outperforms CPU-friendly baselines at tight budgets and remains competitive as budgets scale. Field trials with large aerial and marine robotics data further validate its practicality and scalability: BCVS reduces redundancy up to 16% while sustaining robust SfM registration and high-quality 3D reconstructions.</p></details> | 12 pages |
| **[Feel My Focus: Gaze-Triggered Digital Twin Creation for Remote Collaboration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11640v1)** | 2026-11-08 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present Feel My Focus, a gaze-triggered mixed reality (MR) demonstration that enables rapid creation and sharing of lightweight 3D digital twins during remote collaboration. Unlike systems that use gaze primarily as a referential pointer, Feel My Focus treats sustained visual attention as a generative action: a fixation on a physical object initiates single-image capture, attention-guided segmentation, and one-shot 3D reconstruction, allowing a remote partner to inspect and discuss the object in situ. The system integrates gaze-tracking glasses, modular segmentation and reconstruction services, and an MR client for runtime streaming and interaction. This design prioritizes responsiveness and experiential continuity over photometric fidelity. We report observations from a public demonstration, highlighting design trade-offs, failure modes, and user control considerations for deploying gaze-triggered generative systems in real-world collaborative settings.</p></details> | 5 pages, 3 figures |
| **[TransArt3D: From Two States to Articulated 3D Reconstruction with Video Transition Priors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11304v1)** | 2026-11-08 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Reconstructing interactable articulated assets from a few images is key to robotics and simulation, yet highly underconstrained. Existing optimization-based methods fit an articulated asset under a static 3D prior that scores each state independently, so per-state reconstruction errors leak into the estimated moving part and joint as false motion. Video diffusion models capture how objects move between states, but only implicitly in 2D. To this end, we present TransArt3D, an optimization-based framework that reconstructs an articulated 3D asset from two endpoint images by translating this video transition prior into explicit part structure and joint kinematics. It first lifts frames generated between the endpoints with a transition-coupled 3D consensus that applies the static 3D prior jointly across frames, stabilizing unchanging regions and initializing the parts and joint. A rectified-flow kinematic refinement then lets the video prior supervise a 3D motion field over the whole transition, while part assignment and a shared joint constrain the field throughout optimization, keeping per-state errors out of the articulation. | **[A Straight Line Is Not a Camera Pose: Auditing Short-Clip Pose Benchmarks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00482v2)** | 2026-11-06 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The absolute trajectory error (ATE) is the standard measure of camera-motion accuracy, from SLAM to feed-forward 3D reconstruction. On the common Sintel protocol, an image-free constant-velocity line has a lower mean ATE than 10 of 11 recent models re-run with official code and than 85 of 110 published methods. On Lightspeed it beats 5 of 9 models and 10 of 11 published methods, while on TUM, ScanNet, DyCheck and ADT every model beats it. Two properties of ATE's alignment explain this. First, it maps any moving constant-velocity prediction onto the line that best fits the true path, so the line scores the path's regression residual. A residual signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) criterion tells when moving a prediction towards its constant-velocity fit lowers ATE. With the alignment's first-order refit, it accounts for the outcome of all but one of 4010 model-clip evaluations, including all 504 on three benchmarks registered before any model ran. The true path's straightness flags Sintel and Lightspeed. Second, the alignment ignores where cameras look: rotating a trajectory by 30 degrees against its orientations leaves ATE unchanged yet worsens the static-point reprojection of every model on Sintel, TUM and ScanNet, and the line reprojects worst on Sintel by geometric mean. Fitting the alignment's rotation to the predicted orientations gives pose-anchored ATE (PA-ATE), a one-function change at about ATE's cost (37 vs. 34 $μ$s per clip, and 61 vs. 66 $μ$s with the rotation error). It is never below ATE and, as we prove, equals the translational relative pose error over all frame pairs divided by $\sqrt{2}$ when the orientations agree. It ranks every image-free reference below every model on Sintel, follows the reprojection error where ATE does not, and largely preserves the models' order. | **[Flex-RSI: Benchmarking Recursive Self-Improvement of Frontier Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08946v1)** | 2026-11-06 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Frontier language models are increasingly deployed as agents, and how well an agent performs depends not only on its model but also on its harness: the prompts, tools, memory, and control loop through which the model acts. In each round, an executor improves the task solution, which is kept only if it scores higher on held-out validation data, and an improver then revises the harness, including the executor and itself. | **[GC-MoE on SM120: Overlapping Expert-Parallel Token Dispatch with Warp-Level FP8xFP4 Block-Scaled MMA on Consumer Blackwell](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16219v1)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Expert-parallel Mixture-of-Experts decoding is latency bound: each layer serializes a token exchange, a grouped GEMM over routed experts, and a combine, while only a few tokens per rank are in flight. Prior GC-MoE fused these phases into one persistent, Green-Context-partitioned CUDA Graph on Hopper (SM90) and datacenter Blackwell (SM100). This paper rebuilds the operator for SM120 (compute capability 12.0, e.g. RTX PRO 5000) over PCIe. SM120 exposes block-scaled tensor cores only through warp-level mma.sync.aligned.kind::mxf8f6f4: no tensor memory, no two-CTA MMA, no cluster multicast. We rebuild the kernel around a swap-AB warp-level block-scaled MMA with FP4 weights in the register A operand and FP8 activations in B, plus SM120-specific mechanisms: scale-byte selection through MMA immediates, a compact-weights path, an adaptive token-tile geometry, an expert-granular streaming dispatch with a batched bulk-pull engine and a 64-bit deduplicating pull cache, full-width BF16 returns drained by resident relay copiers over a position-major arena, and shared experts folded into the compute role. On 8xRTX PRO 5000 over PCIe the operator is 1.522x and 1.387x faster than the FlashInfer CuTe DSL W4A8 MegaMoE and the Cake MegaMoE across 18 decode points (18/18 wins each), and 1.388x faster than Cake with shared experts fused; all 94 numerical cases and 752 per-rank checks pass, at cross-operator cosine similarity of at least 0.9999997.</p></details> | 24 pages, 10 tables, 6 figures |

LILA (\emph{Latent-Informed Layer Analysis}) demonstrates that this dependency is avoidable: each FFN weight matrix is factorized with Non-negative Matrix Factorization (NMF) to obtain a parts-based spectral representation, and neuron importance is scored via the Kolmogorov--Smirnov (KS) distance between singular value distributions of the full and neuron-ablated NMF matrix, a closed-form criterion requiring no calibration corpus, forward pass, or gradient computation. Two data-free matrix variants (Abs, Split) generate pruning masks from weight spectral geometry alone; a calibration-guided variant (Act) is provided as an internal ablation. Evaluation spans 7 model families (up to 70B). Without fine-tuning, data-free LILA surpasses PruneNet (45M-parameter RL policy) by up to 2.12~pp in zero-shot accuracy on LLaMA-2-7B and outperforms WikiText-2-calibrated SliceGPT by up to 6.1~pp across all sparsity levels, confirming data-free performance is not an artifact of omitting activation statistics. After one LoRA RFT epoch (matched WikiText-2 protocol), LILA Act surpasses SliceGPT+RFT by 6.39~pp on average using 98.4\% fewer calibration samples (16 vs.\ 1,024) across LLaMA-2-7B and Phi-2. NTK analysis confirms a $17{\times}$ reduction in functional distortion versus random pruning, providing theoretical grounding for the spectral criterion.</p></details> | 31 Pages |

Additional experiments indicate that a moderate number of parents provides beneficial diversity, whereas excessive parent counts, particularly when $p$ is large, lead to excessive population dispersion and hinder convergence.</p></details> |  |
| **[An exact compress-with-another threshold in a binary automaton family](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07044v2)** | 2026-11-10 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>For a state $q$ in a synchronizing automaton, let $μ(q)$ be the minimum length of a word merging $q$ with another initial state. Revised proofs and exposition, corrected boundary transitions, updated references, and added a coauthor |

| **[Branch-Extended Automatic Differentiation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08637v1)** | 2026-11-05 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present branch-extended automatic differentiation (BEAD), a software package that provides information about inactive branches of piecewise-differentiable functions, intended for use in nonsmooth optimization algorithms. | **[A Mode-Unified, Continuous, and Biomimetic Control Strategy of a Robotic Lower Limb Prosthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19183v2)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Existing biomimetic lower-limb prosthesis controllers are commonly designed for discrete locomotion modes, requiring separate parameterizations for level walking, ramps, and stairs. | **[Ab Initio Accuracy Beyond the Cutoff: Distributed Message-Passing Equivariant Attention at Tens-of-Millions-of-Atom Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06122v1)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Molecular dynamics predicts molecular motion from atomic forces and trajectory integration. Density functional theory (DFT) provides first-principles forces, but its $O(N^3)$ cost limits simulations to thousands of atoms; empirical force fields scale to millions but miss bond breaking and charge transfer. Equivariant message-passing Transformers learn the ab initio potential at linear cost. Stacked layers propagate information beyond a single cutoff, giving four layers a receptive field up to 23Å. At this frontier, scale is limited by communication: layers exchange boundary features across devices, force backpropagation repeats the exchange, and moving atoms change neighbor graphs and tensor shapes. We present Tessera, a distributed runtime that fixes workload shapes between rebuilds. It sorts atoms along a space-filling curve into equal-count shards, ensuring identical atom counts and tensor shapes per rank. Peer sets scale as $K^{0.5}$, not $K$. A conservative neighbor skin preserves topology across updates, enabling each rebuild interval to replay as one CUDA Graph. Fused kernels, three-level neighbor search, and overlapped All-to-All complete it. On 512 GPUs, Tessera sustains 30.7M atoms at 89.7% weak-scaling efficiency (77--90% across three per-GPU loads); per-step time rises 1.11--1.29x as machine and problem grow 16x. Graph reuse cuts full-step time $1.87\times$. Across an $8\times$ device increase, neighbor-search cost grows $1.7\times$, versus $3.0\times$ for Morton order. In a live $10.24$M-atom salt solution, NVE energy drift is $0.42\,μ$eV/atom/ps over $10$ ps, and structure matches a serial reference. A 10.06M-atom Norovirus capsid sustains 100 ps of NVT at $\approx 8$ ps/day on 256 A100 GPUs. Over 200,000 steps, it has no broken bonds or anomalously close pairs and uses three compiled variants, with no graph breaks or eager fallbacks.</p></details> |  |
| **[Agentic Behavior Engineering: A Design-by-Contract Methodology for Specifying, Verifying, and Governing Enterprise AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05454v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Enterprise teams that deploy large language model (LLM) agents inherit a maintenance problem that traditional testing does not address. The model is non-deterministic, but the larger risk is that most of what changes an agent's behavior (a retired model version, a revised policy, an upstream schema change, a prompt edit meant to fix one case) originates outside the owning team and is discovered as a production defect rather than caught as a regression. This paper presents Agentic Behavior Engineering (ABE), a platform-agnostic methodology that treats observable agent behavior, rather than the prompt or the model, as the version-controlled engineering artifact. ABE adapts Design by Contract to non-deterministic systems. A specification-only behavior contract with stable rule identifiers states what the agent must do, must not do, and when it must abstain for lack of evidence; a lightweight scenario library turns each rule into concrete situations; and every engineering artifact (tests, runtime policies, telemetry) references the contract in one direction only, so that specification and implementation can change independently without drifting apart. The method organizes the work into five responsibilities (Specify, Ground, Control, Verify, Observe), treats missing evidence and a verified negative as different things, enforces authority outside the model, gates releases on per-scenario pass^k reliability rather than aggregate accuracy, and classifies every production run against the contract so that each finding routes to the asset that must change. We illustrate the method with an executable capacity-allocation boundary suite and a constructed worked example of a product-review approval workflow.</p></details> | 30 pages, 6 figures, 5 tables. Expanded version of the author's practitioner guide "Agentic behavior engineering" in the Microsoft Azure Architecture Center (CC BY 4.0): https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/architecture/ai-ml/guide/agentic-behavior-engineering |
| **[Beyond the Bochner Method: Optimal Spectral Gaps for Block Dynamics of Spin Systems like the $O(N)$ Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05228v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Let $μ$ be a probability distribution on a spin system with $n$ sites and possibly infinite spin spaces. We prove local-to-global spectral-gap bounds for block Glauber dynamics. Given a symmetric, entrywise nonnegative matrix $A$ with zero diagonal and $λ_{\max}(A)&lt;1$ encoding local spectral bounds for a spin system, we derive global spectral-gap bounds for block dynamics on its marginal distributions. | **[GC-MoE on SM120: Overlapping Expert-Parallel Token Dispatch with Warp-Level FP8xFP4 Block-Scaled MMA on Consumer Blackwell](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16219v1)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Expert-parallel Mixture-of-Experts decoding is latency bound: each layer serializes a token exchange, a grouped GEMM over routed experts, and a combine, while only a few tokens per rank are in flight. Prior GC-MoE fused these phases into one persistent, Green-Context-partitioned CUDA Graph on Hopper (SM90) and datacenter Blackwell (SM100). This paper rebuilds the operator for SM120 (compute capability 12.0, e.g. RTX PRO 5000) over PCIe. SM120 exposes block-scaled tensor cores only through warp-level mma.sync.aligned.kind::mxf8f6f4: no tensor memory, no two-CTA MMA, no cluster multicast. We rebuild the kernel around a swap-AB warp-level block-scaled MMA with FP4 weights in the register A operand and FP8 activations in B, plus SM120-specific mechanisms: scale-byte selection through MMA immediates, a compact-weights path, an adaptive token-tile geometry, an expert-granular streaming dispatch with a batched bulk-pull engine and a 64-bit deduplicating pull cache, full-width BF16 returns drained by resident relay copiers over a position-major arena, and shared experts folded into the compute role. On 8xRTX PRO 5000 over PCIe the operator is 1.522x and 1.387x faster than the FlashInfer CuTe DSL W4A8 MegaMoE and the Cake MegaMoE across 18 decode points (18/18 wins each), and 1.388x faster than Cake with shared experts fused; all 94 numerical cases and 752 per-rank checks pass, at cross-operator cosine similarity of at least 0.9999997.</p></details> | 24 pages, 10 tables, 6 figures |

Evaluation spans 7 model families (up to 70B). Without fine-tuning, data-free LILA surpasses PruneNet (45M-parameter RL policy) by up to 2.12~pp in zero-shot accuracy on LLaMA-2-7B and outperforms WikiText-2-calibrated SliceGPT by up to 6.1~pp across all sparsity levels, confirming data-free performance is not an artifact of omitting activation statistics. After one LoRA RFT epoch (matched WikiText-2 protocol), LILA Act surpasses SliceGPT+RFT by 6.39~pp on average using 98.4\% fewer calibration samples (16 vs.\ 1,024) across LLaMA-2-7B and Phi-2. | **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |
| **[Martine: Benchmarking Multi-View 3D Surface Reconstruction Across Viewpoint Coverage, Resolution, and Lighting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11602v1)** | 2026-11-08 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Controlled acquisition setups are widely used for high-accuracy multi-view 3D surface reconstruction, as they allow sensor configuration, image resolution, viewpoint distribution, and illumination to be specified. Yet benchmarking remains difficult, since reconstruction accuracy depends jointly on the reconstruction paradigm, object properties, and acquisition regime. Existing benchmarks often restrict viewpoint coverage, resolution, or illumination, which can bias comparisons towards methods suited to the chosen setting. Others lack accurate 3D ground truth or quantified image-to-geometry alignment, limiting their use for metric surface evaluation. We introduce Martine, a benchmark for controlled 3D object surface reconstruction. Martine comprises 28 objects, including transparent materials and fine structures, captured at 9568 x 6376 pixels from 84 views over a spherical cap under both single-light and multi-light regimes. It provides object-independent camera calibration, accurate 3D ground-truth scans, calibration and image-to-geometry accuracy quantified on test images, and 3D property masks for analysing errors by material properties, visibility, curvature and fine structures. Experiments across photogrammetry, classical multi-view stereo, neural implicit surfaces, Gaussian-splatting-based reconstruction, and multi-view photometric stereo show that Martine enables regime-aware evaluation with explicit control over reference-data accuracy. The benchmark resolves sub-millimetric reconstruction differences, with a best observed Chamfer distance of 0.058 mm over the evaluated visible surface.</p></details> | Accepted at the 40th Conference on Neural Information Processing Systems (NeurIPS 2026), Track on Evaluations and Datasets. 23 pages, 14 figures, 8 tables. Project page: https://martine-benchmark.github.io/ |
| **[Active Learning for Gaussian Process Regression Under Self-Induced Boltzmann Weights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10654v2)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We consider the active learning problem where the goal is to learn an unknown function with low prediction error under an unknown Boltzmann distribution induced by the function itself. This self-induced weighting arises naturally in problems such as potential energy surface (PES) modeling in computational chemistry, yet poses unique challenges as the target distribution is unknown and its partition function is intractable. We propose \texttt{AB-SID-iVAR}, a Gaussian Process-based acquisition function that approximates the intractable Bayesian target distribution in closed form while avoiding partition function estimation, and is applicable to both discrete and continuous input domains. We also analyze a Thompson sampling alternative (\texttt{TS-SID-iVAR}) as a higher variance Monte Carlo variant. Despite the unknown target, under mild conditions, we establish that the terminal prediction error vanishes with high probability, and provide a tighter average-case guarantee. We demonstrate consistent improvements over existing approaches in this setting on synthetic benchmarks and real-world PES modeling and drug discovery tasks. Our code is available at https://github.com/TsingQAQ/SIDAL</p></details> |  |
| **[Singular Bayesian Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.00387v5)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Bayesian neural networks promise calibrated uncertainty but require $O(mn)$ parameters for standard mean-field Gaussian posteriors. By parameterizing weights as $W = AB^{\top}$ with $A \in \mathbb{R}^{m \times r}$, $B \in \mathbb{R}^{n \times r}$, we induce a posterior that is \emph{singular} with respect to the Lebesgue measure, concentrating on the rank-$r$ manifold. This singularity captures structured weight correlations through shared latent factors, geometrically distinct from mean-field's independence assumption. We derive PAC-Bayes generalization bounds whose complexity term scales as $\sqrt{r(m+n)}$ instead of $\sqrt{m n}$, and prove loss bounds that decompose the error into optimization and rank-induced bias using the Eckart-Young-Mirsky theorem. We further adapt recent Gaussian complexity bounds for low-rank deterministic networks to Bayesian predictive means. Empirically, across MLPs, LSTMs, and Transformers on standard benchmarks, our method achieves competitive predictive performance while using up to $33\times$ fewer parameters than 5-member Deep Ensembles. It substantially improves OOD detection and often improves calibration relative to mean-field and perturbation baselines, while Deep Ensembles can still be stronger on in-distribution likelihood-based metrics.</p></details> | 8 pages Main text, 53 pages Appendix, 20 figures Proceedings of the 43 rd International Conference on Machine Learning (ICML 2026) |
| **[Branch-Extended Automatic Differentiation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08637v1)** | 2026-11-05 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present branch-extended automatic differentiation (BEAD), a software package that provides information about inactive branches of piecewise-differentiable functions, intended for use in nonsmooth optimization algorithms. For supported nonsmooth operations such as abs and max, BEAD identifies alternative branches within tolerance from the given input, and computes gradients of the resulting branches extended to the given input. Our experiments show that our approach is as accurate as manually written derivative code, and offers more flexibility in modifying the function and choosing between branch-discovery rules. We categorize the objects into three regimes: objects fixed at one site, objects that move between sites, and designs shared by many physical units. Evaluating recent feedforward reconstruction models on Trainspotting shows that they fail most severely in the last two regimes. Fine-tuning VGGT-$Ω$ on Trainspotting significantly improves camera pose, depth, and 3D geometry on held-out objects. | **[Ab Initio Accuracy Beyond the Cutoff: Distributed Message-Passing Equivariant Attention at Tens-of-Millions-of-Atom Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06122v1)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Molecular dynamics predicts molecular motion from atomic forces and trajectory integration. Density functional theory (DFT) provides first-principles forces, but its $O(N^3)$ cost limits simulations to thousands of atoms; empirical force fields scale to millions but miss bond breaking and charge transfer. Equivariant message-passing Transformers learn the ab initio potential at linear cost. Stacked layers propagate information beyond a single cutoff, giving four layers a receptive field up to 23Å. At this frontier, scale is limited by communication: layers exchange boundary features across devices, force backpropagation repeats the exchange, and moving atoms change neighbor graphs and tensor shapes. We present Tessera, a distributed runtime that fixes workload shapes between rebuilds. It sorts atoms along a space-filling curve into equal-count shards, ensuring identical atom counts and tensor shapes per rank. Peer sets scale as $K^{0.5}$, not $K$. A conservative neighbor skin preserves topology across updates, enabling each rebuild interval to replay as one CUDA Graph. Fused kernels, three-level neighbor search, and overlapped All-to-All complete it. On 512 GPUs, Tessera sustains 30.7M atoms at 89.7% weak-scaling efficiency (77--90% across three per-GPU loads); per-step time rises 1.11--1.29x as machine and problem grow 16x. Graph reuse cuts full-step time $1.87\times$. Across an $8\times$ device increase, neighbor-search cost grows $1.7\times$, versus $3.0\times$ for Morton order. In a live $10.24$M-atom salt solution, NVE energy drift is $0.42\,μ$eV/atom/ps over $10$ ps, and structure matches a serial reference. A 10.06M-atom Norovirus capsid sustains 100 ps of NVT at $\approx 8$ ps/day on 256 A100 GPUs. Over 200,000 steps, it has no broken bonds or anomalously close pairs and uses three compiled variants, with no graph breaks or eager fallbacks.</p></details> |  |
| **[Agentic Behavior Engineering: A Design-by-Contract Methodology for Specifying, Verifying, and Governing Enterprise AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05454v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Enterprise teams that deploy large language model (LLM) agents inherit a maintenance problem that traditional testing does not address. The method organizes the work into five responsibilities (Specify, Ground, Control, Verify, Observe), treats missing evidence and a verified negative as different things, enforces authority outside the model, gates releases on per-scenario pass^k reliability rather than aggregate accuracy, and classifies every production run against the contract so that each finding routes to the asset that must change. | **[Track-Free Dense Structure from Motion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.20858v3)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Classical Bundle Adjustment (BA) relies on precise metric initialization and known camera intrinsics, and traditional Structure-from-Motion (SfM) pipelines struggle to exploit modern dense matchers because rigid track-building heuristics break down under their inherent noise. On 512 GPUs, Tessera sustains 30.7M atoms at 89.7% weak-scaling efficiency (77--90% across three per-GPU loads); per-step time rises 1.11--1.29x as machine and problem grow 16x. | **[MedGS: Gaussian Splatting for Multi-Modal 3D Medical Imaging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.16806v5)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multi-modal three-dimensional (3D) medical imaging data, derived from ultrasound, magnetic resonance imaging (MRI), and computed tomography (CT), provide a widely adopted approach for non-invasive anatomical visualization. To address these challenges, we present \our{}, a novel framework based on Gaussian Splatting (GS) designed for high-fidelity 3D anatomical reconstruction. Uniquely, \our{} employs a multi-task architecture that simultaneously performs frame interpolation and segmentation using a unified geometric representation. By coupling these tasks, the model leverages dense signals from image synthesis to regularize the geometry, enabling high-quality surface extraction even from a limited number of input frames. Specifically, medical data are modeled as Folded-Gaussians with dual color attributes, supported by an In-Between Frame Regularization (IBFR) mechanism. | **[GC-MoE on SM120: Overlapping Expert-Parallel Token Dispatch with Warp-Level FP8xFP4 Block-Scaled MMA on Consumer Blackwell](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16219v1)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Expert-parallel Mixture-of-Experts decoding is latency bound: each layer serializes a token exchange, a grouped GEMM over routed experts, and a combine, while only a few tokens per rank are in flight. SM120 exposes block-scaled tensor cores only through warp-level mma.sync.aligned.kind::mxf8f6f4: no tensor memory, no two-CTA MMA, no cluster multicast. | **[Ab Initio Accuracy Beyond the Cutoff: Distributed Message-Passing Equivariant Attention at Tens-of-Millions-of-Atom Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06122v1)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Molecular dynamics predicts molecular motion from atomic forces and trajectory integration. Density functional theory (DFT) provides first-principles forces, but its $O(N^3)$ cost limits simulations to thousands of atoms; empirical force fields scale to millions but miss bond breaking and charge transfer. Equivariant message-passing Transformers learn the ab initio potential at linear cost. Stacked layers propagate information beyond a single cutoff, giving four layers a receptive field up to 23Å. At this frontier, scale is limited by communication: layers exchange boundary features across devices, force backpropagation repeats the exchange, and moving atoms change neighbor graphs and tensor shapes. We present Tessera, a distributed runtime that fixes workload shapes between rebuilds. It sorts atoms along a space-filling curve into equal-count shards, ensuring identical atom counts and tensor shapes per rank. Peer sets scale as $K^{0.5}$, not $K$. A conservative neighbor skin preserves topology across updates, enabling each rebuild interval to replay as one CUDA Graph. Fused kernels, three-level neighbor search, and overlapped All-to-All complete it. On 512 GPUs, Tessera sustains 30.7M atoms at 89.7% weak-scaling efficiency (77--90% across three per-GPU loads); per-step time rises 1.11--1.29x as machine and problem grow 16x. Graph reuse cuts full-step time $1.87\times$. Across an $8\times$ device increase, neighbor-search cost grows $1.7\times$, versus $3.0\times$ for Morton order. In a live $10.24$M-atom salt solution, NVE energy drift is $0.42\,μ$eV/atom/ps over $10$ ps, and structure matches a serial reference. A 10.06M-atom Norovirus capsid sustains 100 ps of NVT at $\approx 8$ ps/day on 256 A100 GPUs. Over 200,000 steps, it has no broken bonds or anomalously close pairs and uses three compiled variants, with no graph breaks or eager fallbacks.</p></details> |  |

## neural rendering
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **[Neural Render Proxies for Interactive and Differentiable Lighting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17185v1)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Within the CG animation production pipeline, the challenges artists tackle in Lighting can be immense. Even minor adjustments require re-rendering massive scenes with slow offline renderers; global illumination has to be sampled and complex shaders have to be evaluated, leading to iteration times of minutes to hours per frame. To accelerate this process, we introduce a novel neural render proxy (NRP) that enables differentiable relighting of static scenes with fixed camera and materials at interactive rates. Our main insight is the decoupling of traditional rendering into path sampling and emission computation. From a single, light-agnostic render pass, we collect light transport data in the form of path samples. This enables rapidly sampling lighting conditions on the fly, and training a scene-specific lightweight neural network that learns how light is transported from any scene location to any image pixel. This approach is compatible with non-differentiable production renderers, induces minimal memory requirements during inference, and scales only with resolution and the number of light sources and parameters, but independently of scene and appearance complexity. Our evaluation demonstrates interactive frame rates for relighting (~30-60 Hz) while closely approximating the visual fidelity of ground-truth path tracing. In addition, the differentiable NRP enables fast, gradient-based inverse workflows, allowing artists to efficiently solve for lighting parameters from intuitive image-space edits or generative targets.</p></details> | Accepted at Computer Graphics Forum (Proc. EGSR 2026) |
| **[GC-MoE on SM120: Overlapping Expert-Parallel Token Dispatch with Warp-Level FP8xFP4 Block-Scaled MMA on Consumer Blackwell](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16219v1)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Expert-parallel Mixture-of-Experts decoding is latency bound: each layer serializes a token exchange, a grouped GEMM over routed experts, and a combine, while only a few tokens per rank are in flight. Prior GC-MoE fused these phases into one persistent, Green-Context-partitioned CUDA Graph on Hopper (SM90) and datacenter Blackwell (SM100). This paper rebuilds the operator for SM120 (compute capability 12.0, e.g. RTX PRO 5000) over PCIe. SM120 exposes block-scaled tensor cores only through warp-level mma.sync.aligned.kind::mxf8f6f4: no tensor memory, no two-CTA MMA, no cluster multicast. We rebuild the kernel around a swap-AB warp-level block-scaled MMA with FP4 weights in the register A operand and FP8 activations in B, plus SM120-specific mechanisms: scale-byte selection through MMA immediates, a compact-weights path, an adaptive token-tile geometry, an expert-granular streaming dispatch with a batched bulk-pull engine and a 64-bit deduplicating pull cache, full-width BF16 returns drained by resident relay copiers over a position-major arena, and shared experts folded into the compute role. On 8xRTX PRO 5000 over PCIe the operator is 1.522x and 1.387x faster than the FlashInfer CuTe DSL W4A8 MegaMoE and the Cake MegaMoE across 18 decode points (18/18 wins each), and 1.388x faster than Cake with shared experts fused; all 94 numerical cases and 752 per-rank checks pass, at cross-operator cosine similarity of at least 0.9999997.</p></details> | 24 pages, 10 tables, 6 figures |
| **[LILA: Data-Free Structured Pruning of LLMs via Latent Spectral Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11163v2)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Structured pruning of large language models (LLMs) has universally required calibration data, gradient computation, or large auxiliary policy networks at pruning time. Two data-free matrix variants (Abs, Split) generate pruning masks from weight spectral geometry alone; a calibration-guided variant (Act) is provided as an internal ablation. Evaluation spans 7 model families (up to 70B). Without fine-tuning, data-free LILA surpasses PruneNet (45M-parameter RL policy) by up to 2.12~pp in zero-shot accuracy on LLaMA-2-7B and outperforms WikiText-2-calibrated SliceGPT by up to 6.1~pp across all sparsity levels, confirming data-free performance is not an artifact of omitting activation statistics. After one LoRA RFT epoch (matched WikiText-2 protocol), LILA Act surpasses SliceGPT+RFT by 6.39~pp on average using 98.4\% fewer calibration samples (16 vs.\ 1,024) across LLaMA-2-7B and Phi-2. NTK analysis confirms a $17{\times}$ reduction in functional distortion versus random pruning, providing theoretical grounding for the spectral criterion.</p></details> | 31 Pages |
| **[A Multi-Parent Edge Assembly Crossover Driven Hybrid Genetic Algorithm for the Hamiltonian \textit{p}-Median Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15219v1)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The Hamiltonian \textit{p}-median problem seeks to partition the vertices of a graph into exactly $p$ disjoint cycles while minimizing their total cost. | **[Ab Initio Accuracy Beyond the Cutoff: Distributed Message-Passing Equivariant Attention at Tens-of-Millions-of-Atom Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06122v1)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Molecular dynamics predicts molecular motion from atomic forces and trajectory integration. Density functional theory (DFT) provides first-principles forces, but its $O(N^3)$ cost limits simulations to thousands of atoms; empirical force fields scale to millions but miss bond breaking and charge transfer. Equivariant message-passing Transformers learn the ab initio potential at linear cost. Stacked layers propagate information beyond a single cutoff, giving four layers a receptive field up to 23Å. At this frontier, scale is limited by communication: layers exchange boundary features across devices, force backpropagation repeats the exchange, and moving atoms change neighbor graphs and tensor shapes. We present Tessera, a distributed runtime that fixes workload shapes between rebuilds. It sorts atoms along a space-filling curve into equal-count shards, ensuring identical atom counts and tensor shapes per rank. Peer sets scale as $K^{0.5}$, not $K$. A conservative neighbor skin preserves topology across updates, enabling each rebuild interval to replay as one CUDA Graph. Fused kernels, three-level neighbor search, and overlapped All-to-All complete it. On 512 GPUs, Tessera sustains 30.7M atoms at 89.7% weak-scaling efficiency (77--90% across three per-GPU loads); per-step time rises 1.11--1.29x as machine and problem grow 16x. The method organizes the work into five responsibilities (Specify, Ground, Control, Verify, Observe), treats missing evidence and a verified negative as different things, enforces authority outside the model, gates releases on per-scenario pass^k reliability rather than aggregate accuracy, and classifies every production run against the contract so that each finding routes to the asset that must change. We also obtain spectral bounds for swap walks and local spectral expansion of partite simplicial complexes. This bound is independent of the number and sizes of each group, and is nontrivial for every prime power $q$. Finally, for random forests $F$ of any finite graph with arbitrary positive edge weights, we prove that \[ \Pr(e,f\in F)\le \frac{4}{3} \Pr(e\in F)\Pr(f\in F) \] for distinct edges $e,f$, improving the factor 2 supplied by general log-concavity bounds for matroid independent sets. The argument combines our extended Bochner method with the analysis of an auxiliary real stable polynomial.</p></details> | 42 pages |

| **[GC-MoE on SM120: Overlapping Expert-Parallel Token Dispatch with Warp-Level FP8xFP4 Block-Scaled MMA on Consumer Blackwell](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16219v1)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Expert-parallel Mixture-of-Experts decoding is latency bound: each layer serializes a token exchange, a grouped GEMM over routed experts, and a combine, while only a few tokens per rank are in flight. Prior GC-MoE fused these phases into one persistent, Green-Context-partitioned CUDA Graph on Hopper (SM90) and datacenter Blackwell (SM100). This paper rebuilds the operator for SM120 (compute capability 12.0, e.g. RTX PRO 5000) over PCIe. SM120 exposes block-scaled tensor cores only through warp-level mma.sync.aligned.kind::mxf8f6f4: no tensor memory, no two-CTA MMA, no cluster multicast. We rebuild the kernel around a swap-AB warp-level block-scaled MMA with FP4 weights in the register A operand and FP8 activations in B, plus SM120-specific mechanisms: scale-byte selection through MMA immediates, a compact-weights path, an adaptive token-tile geometry, an expert-granular streaming dispatch with a batched bulk-pull engine and a 64-bit deduplicating pull cache, full-width BF16 returns drained by resident relay copiers over a position-major arena, and shared experts folded into the compute role. On 8xRTX PRO 5000 over PCIe the operator is 1.522x and 1.387x faster than the FlashInfer CuTe DSL W4A8 MegaMoE and the Cake MegaMoE across 18 decode points (18/18 wins each), and 1.388x faster than Cake with shared experts fused; all 94 numerical cases and 752 per-rank checks pass, at cross-operator cosine similarity of at least 0.9999997.</p></details> | 24 pages, 10 tables, 6 figures |

Two data-free matrix variants (Abs, Split) generate pruning masks from weight spectral geometry alone; a calibration-guided variant (Act) is provided as an internal ablation. Evaluation spans 7 model families (up to 70B). Without fine-tuning, data-free LILA surpasses PruneNet (45M-parameter RL policy) by up to 2.12~pp in zero-shot accuracy on LLaMA-2-7B and outperforms WikiText-2-calibrated SliceGPT by up to 6.1~pp across all sparsity levels, confirming data-free performance is not an artifact of omitting activation statistics. After one LoRA RFT epoch (matched WikiText-2 protocol), LILA Act surpasses SliceGPT+RFT by 6.39~pp on average using 98.4\% fewer calibration samples (16 vs.\ 1,024) across LLaMA-2-7B and Phi-2. | **[MatrixSplat: Matrix-Parallel and Feature-Scalable 3D Gaussian Splatting Rendering on GPUs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15153v1)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) has emerged as a mainstream representation for real-time novel view synthesis and 3D reconstruction. The complex-valued output makes the renderer product-agnostic. This is between 1.7x and 5.2x the three optical-NVS baselines (RadarSplat, Radar Fields, DART) and 1.3x the mmIR ray tracer at 1/25 of its wall-clock time. Project page: https://3d-point-splatting.github.io/ |

| **[DPGS-SLAM: Diffusion-Prior and Provenance-Aware Monocular Gaussian Splatting SLAM for Dynamic Visual Sensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12038v2)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents DPGS-SLAM (Diffusion-Prior and Provenance-Aware Monocular Gaussian Splatting SLAM) for dense mapping in environments containing dynamic objects. Objects in dynamic motion remain a significant challenge for dense visual SLAM since dynamic observations can contaminate camera tracking and scene reconstruction. Although 3DGS (3D Gaussian splatting) enables efficient dense mapping, existing 3DGS-SLAM systems may exhibit instability when dynamic objects violate their underlying static-scene assumption. Excluding dynamic observations can reduce their influence on tracking and mapping, but may leave occluded static backgrounds missing or weakly constrained. Our proposed DPGS-SLAM is a monocular RGB SLAM framework that supports provisional representations of insufficiently observed static backgrounds through asynchronous diffusion-based background completion and provenance-aware Gaussian mapping. Dynamic regions are identified through optical-flow-based motion discovery and temporally consistent mask propagation by SAM2. For selected keyframes, a fine-tuned diffusion inpainting model generates plausible background appearance in occluded regions, while metric depth predicted from completed images initializes provisional Gaussian primitives. Provenance labels distinguish generated primitives from observation-supported primitives, excluding generated content from camera-pose estimation and observed-map optimization. When previously occluded backgrounds become visible, eligible generated primitives are removed, and observation-supported primitives are independently initialized from real RGB observations and predicted depth. Experimental results demonstrated competitive camera tracking, with average ATE RMSE values of 1.2 cm on TUM RGB-D and 2.7 cm on BONN RGB-D datasets. This paper rebuilds the operator for SM120 (compute capability 12.0, e.g. RTX PRO 5000) over PCIe. SM120 exposes block-scaled tensor cores only through warp-level mma.sync.aligned.kind::mxf8f6f4: no tensor memory, no two-CTA MMA, no cluster multicast. RF3DGS exclusively leverages a widely-adopted photometric prior for reflection removal: that the minimum pixel intensity across multi-view images corresponding to the same 3D point strongly correlates with reflection-free information. However, generalizing this principle to a 3D Gaussian Splatting within a multi-view context is non-trivial. To address this, RF3DGS first optimizes a vanilla 3DGS model using a geometric prior to estimate a coarse, reflection-free depth map for each view. Leveraging these depth maps and photometric prior, a 3D warping process aggregates reflection-free information from auxiliary views to construct a clean transmission pseudo-label for a given reference view. This pseudo-label provides a robust self-supervised signal for optimization. We further introduce an Adaptive Twin Rendering (ATR) loss to synergistically utilize this pseudo-label while preserving high-frequency details from the original, reflection-degraded images. By adeptly fusing these internal 3D self-priors with the ATR loss, RF3DGS demonstrates state-of-the-art reflection removal performance across both synthetic and real-world scenarios. | **[SplatLab: Autonomous Scientific Discovery for 3DGS Research](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.30468v3)** | 2026-11-10 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Scientific discovery requires more than generating hypotheses: ideas must be grounded in prior work, translated into executable methods, experimentally validated, challenged by counterevidence, and communicated through reproducible findings. A cascade-weighted Elo tournament evolves candidate methods using measured GPU performance and verbal-gradient feedback, while a standing falsifier incorporates adversarial stress testing directly into selection. A shortest word merging $q_0$ with another state is $(ba)^p(ab)^p$. We also construct a reset word of length $3p^2+4p-1$. From the physics-awareness perspective, we introduce a three-level integration framework encompassing data-level feature engineering, loss-level partial differential equation regularization, and \added{architecture-level} structural isomorphism. Refined masks alone substantially improve competing methods, yet a residual gap in label-geometry coupling persists that our fixed-identity design eliminates.
