| **[An exact compress-with-another threshold in a binary automaton family](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07044v2)** | 2026-11-10 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>For a state $q$ in a synchronizing automaton, let $μ(q)$ be the minimum length of a word merging $q$ with another initial state. Szykuła asked whether $μ(q)=O(n)$ for strongly connected synchronizing automata and described a binary family attributed to Dżyga, with $n=3p$ states and a conjectured threshold $4n/3$. We specify the boundary transitions omitted from the published piecewise definition and prove that the resulting automata $\mathcal A_p$ satisfy $μ(q_0)=4p=4n/3$ for all $p\geq3$. A shortest word merging $q_0$ with another state is $(ba)^p(ab)^p$. We also construct a reset word of length $3p^2+4p-1$. Consequently, any uniform linear upper bound for $μ(q)$ on binary strongly connected synchronizing automata with corank-one letters has leading constant at least $4/3$.</p></details> | 9 pages, 1 figure. Revised proofs and exposition, corrected boundary transitions, updated references, and added a coauthor |
| **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset comprises 770 RANS simulations of 194 unique wing configurations, sampled across 27 airfoil profile variants and 4 distances from the ground, spanning variations in wing sweep, chord, taper, angle of attack, and tip twist. Six neural network architectures representing distinct paradigms --- convolutional (DoMINO), graph-based (X-MeshGraphNet), transformer-based (AB-UPT, GeoTransolver, GAOT), and JEPA-based (AeroJEPA) --- are evaluated on their ability to predict surface pressure coefficient and wall shear stress distributions, from which integral drag and lift coefficients are recovered. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |
| **[Active Learning for Gaussian Process Regression Under Self-Induced Boltzmann Weights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10654v2)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We consider the active learning problem where the goal is to learn an unknown function with low prediction error under an unknown Boltzmann distribution induced by the function itself. This self-induced weighting arises naturally in problems such as potential energy surface (PES) modeling in computational chemistry, yet poses unique challenges as the target distribution is unknown and its partition function is intractable. We propose \texttt{AB-SID-iVAR}, a Gaussian Process-based acquisition function that approximates the intractable Bayesian target distribution in closed form while avoiding partition function estimation, and is applicable to both discrete and continuous input domains. | **[Ab Initio Accuracy Beyond the Cutoff: Distributed Message-Passing Equivariant Attention at Tens-of-Millions-of-Atom Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06122v1)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Molecular dynamics predicts molecular motion from atomic forces and trajectory integration. A conservative neighbor skin preserves topology across updates, enabling each rebuild interval to replay as one CUDA Graph. Fused kernels, three-level neighbor search, and overlapped All-to-All complete it. On 512 GPUs, Tessera sustains 30.7M atoms at 89.7% weak-scaling efficiency (77--90% across three per-GPU loads); per-step time rises 1.11--1.29x as machine and problem grow 16x. | **[Agentic Behavior Engineering: A Design-by-Contract Methodology for Specifying, Verifying, and Governing Enterprise AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05454v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Enterprise teams that deploy large language model (LLM) agents inherit a maintenance problem that traditional testing does not address. | **[Beyond the Bochner Method: Optimal Spectral Gaps for Block Dynamics of Spin Systems like the $O(N)$ Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05228v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Let $μ$ be a probability distribution on a spin system with $n$ sites and possibly infinite spin spaces. We also obtain spectral bounds for swap walks and local spectral expansion of partite simplicial complexes. Finally, for random forests $F$ of any finite graph with arbitrary positive edge weights, we prove that \[ \Pr(e,f\in F)\le \frac{4}{3} \Pr(e\in F)\Pr(f\in F) \] for distinct edges $e,f$, improving the factor 2 supplied by general log-concavity bounds for matroid independent sets. | **[GC-MoE on SM120: Overlapping Expert-Parallel Token Dispatch with Warp-Level FP8xFP4 Block-Scaled MMA on Consumer Blackwell](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16219v1)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Expert-parallel Mixture-of-Experts decoding is latency bound: each layer serializes a token exchange, a grouped GEMM over routed experts, and a combine, while only a few tokens per rank are in flight. Prior GC-MoE fused these phases into one persistent, Green-Context-partitioned CUDA Graph on Hopper (SM90) and datacenter Blackwell (SM100). This paper rebuilds the operator for SM120 (compute capability 12.0, e.g. RTX PRO 5000) over PCIe. SM120 exposes block-scaled tensor cores only through warp-level mma.sync.aligned.kind::mxf8f6f4: no tensor memory, no two-CTA MMA, no cluster multicast. We rebuild the kernel around a swap-AB warp-level block-scaled MMA with FP4 weights in the register A operand and FP8 activations in B, plus SM120-specific mechanisms: scale-byte selection through MMA immediates, a compact-weights path, an adaptive token-tile geometry, an expert-granular streaming dispatch with a batched bulk-pull engine and a 64-bit deduplicating pull cache, full-width BF16 returns drained by resident relay copiers over a position-major arena, and shared experts folded into the compute role. On 8xRTX PRO 5000 over PCIe the operator is 1.522x and 1.387x faster than the FlashInfer CuTe DSL W4A8 MegaMoE and the Cake MegaMoE across 18 decode points (18/18 wins each), and 1.388x faster than Cake with shared experts fused; all 94 numerical cases and 752 per-rank checks pass, at cross-operator cosine similarity of at least 0.9999997.</p></details> | 24 pages, 10 tables, 6 figures |

Two data-free matrix variants (Abs, Split) generate pruning masks from weight spectral geometry alone; a calibration-guided variant (Act) is provided as an internal ablation. Evaluation spans 7 model families (up to 70B). Without fine-tuning, data-free LILA surpasses PruneNet (45M-parameter RL policy) by up to 2.12~pp in zero-shot accuracy on LLaMA-2-7B and outperforms WikiText-2-calibrated SliceGPT by up to 6.1~pp across all sparsity levels, confirming data-free performance is not an artifact of omitting activation statistics. After one LoRA RFT epoch (matched WikiText-2 protocol), LILA Act surpasses SliceGPT+RFT by 6.39~pp on average using 98.4\% fewer calibration samples (16 vs.\ 1,024) across LLaMA-2-7B and Phi-2. NTK analysis confirms a $17{\times}$ reduction in functional distortion versus random pruning, providing theoretical grounding for the spectral criterion.</p></details> | 31 Pages |

| **[An exact compress-with-another threshold in a binary automaton family](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07044v2)** | 2026-11-10 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>For a state $q$ in a synchronizing automaton, let $μ(q)$ be the minimum length of a word merging $q$ with another initial state. | **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset comprises 770 RANS simulations of 194 unique wing configurations, sampled across 27 airfoil profile variants and 4 distances from the ground, spanning variations in wing sweep, chord, taper, angle of attack, and tip twist. Six neural network architectures representing distinct paradigms --- convolutional (DoMINO), graph-based (X-MeshGraphNet), transformer-based (AB-UPT, GeoTransolver, GAOT), and JEPA-based (AeroJEPA) --- are evaluated on their ability to predict surface pressure coefficient and wall shear stress distributions, from which integral drag and lift coefficients are recovered. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |
| **[Active Learning for Gaussian Process Regression Under Self-Induced Boltzmann Weights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10654v2)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We consider the active learning problem where the goal is to learn an unknown function with low prediction error under an unknown Boltzmann distribution induced by the function itself. This self-induced weighting arises naturally in problems such as potential energy surface (PES) modeling in computational chemistry, yet poses unique challenges as the target distribution is unknown and its partition function is intractable. We propose \texttt{AB-SID-iVAR}, a Gaussian Process-based acquisition function that approximates the intractable Bayesian target distribution in closed form while avoiding partition function estimation, and is applicable to both discrete and continuous input domains. We also analyze a Thompson sampling alternative (\texttt{TS-SID-iVAR}) as a higher variance Monte Carlo variant. Despite the unknown target, under mild conditions, we establish that the terminal prediction error vanishes with high probability, and provide a tighter average-case guarantee. We demonstrate consistent improvements over existing approaches in this setting on synthetic benchmarks and real-world PES modeling and drug discovery tasks. Our code is available at https://github.com/TsingQAQ/SIDAL</p></details> |  |
| **[Singular Bayesian Neural Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.00387v5)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Bayesian neural networks promise calibrated uncertainty but require $O(mn)$ parameters for standard mean-field Gaussian posteriors. We argue this cost is often unnecessary, particularly when weight matrices exhibit fast singular value decay. By parameterizing weights as $W = AB^{\top}$ with $A \in \mathbb{R}^{m \times r}$, $B \in \mathbb{R}^{n \times r}$, we induce a posterior that is \emph{singular} with respect to the Lebesgue measure, concentrating on the rank-$r$ manifold. This singularity captures structured weight correlations through shared latent factors, geometrically distinct from mean-field's independence assumption. We derive PAC-Bayes generalization bounds whose complexity term scales as $\sqrt{r(m+n)}$ instead of $\sqrt{m n}$, and prove loss bounds that decompose the error into optimization and rank-induced bias using the Eckart-Young-Mirsky theorem. We further adapt recent Gaussian complexity bounds for low-rank deterministic networks to Bayesian predictive means. Empirically, across MLPs, LSTMs, and Transformers on standard benchmarks, our method achieves competitive predictive performance while using up to $33\times$ fewer parameters than 5-member Deep Ensembles. It substantially improves OOD detection and often improves calibration relative to mean-field and perturbation baselines, while Deep Ensembles can still be stronger on in-distribution likelihood-based metrics.</p></details> | 8 pages Main text, 53 pages Appendix, 20 figures Proceedings of the 43 rd International Conference on Machine Learning (ICML 2026) |
| **[Branch-Extended Automatic Differentiation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08637v1)** | 2026-11-05 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We present branch-extended automatic differentiation (BEAD), a software package that provides information about inactive branches of piecewise-differentiable functions, intended for use in nonsmooth optimization algorithms. BEAD is implemented in Python, building on the popular JAX framework. For supported nonsmooth operations such as abs and max, BEAD identifies alternative branches within tolerance from the given input, and computes gradients of the resulting branches extended to the given input. Such information has been previously used for robust optimization methods, but has required error-prone and tedious manual differentiation, unlike BEAD, which is fully automated for functions written in NumPy-like JAX code. Our experiments show that our approach is as accurate as manually written derivative code, and offers more flexibility in modifying the function and choosing between branch-discovery rules. While we were originally motivated by the manifold sampling algorithm for nonsmooth composite objectives, we also integrate BEAD branch-extension gradients into a nonsmooth BFGS-SQP solver. On two small nonsmooth test problems, the resulting path-aware search resolves failures or substantially reduces the number of evaluations required by the BFGS-SQP solver.</p></details> |  |
| **[A Mode-Unified, Continuous, and Biomimetic Control Strategy of a Robotic Lower Limb Prosthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19183v2)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Existing biomimetic lower-limb prosthesis controllers are commonly designed for discrete locomotion modes, requiring separate parameterizations for level walking, ramps, and stairs. We present a mode-unified prosthesis control framework that represents knee and ankle biomechanics continuously as functions of terrain slope and gait phase across those locomotion conditions. Locally optimized ascent and descent polynomial models were unified using radial basis function (RBF) interpolation, preserving the accuracy of the partitioned models while outperforming a global polynomial representation. The resulting biomechanics were transformed into continuous and slope-dependent impedance parameters and implemented within a single finite-state-machine (FSM) controller using bilateral sensing for gait-state estimation. Experiments with 10 individuals with transfemoral amputation demonstrated terrain-adaptive locomotion from level walking through ramps and stairs up to 35 degrees, including 13 degree ramp and 15 degree stair conditions absent from the underlying able-bodied (AB) dataset. Experimental results reproduced the principal slope-dependent biomechanical trends predicted by the unified model, with high agreement for knee-related measures (r-square = 0.82 - 0.99) and ankle kinematics (r-square= 0.78 - 0.96). These findings demonstrated that unified biomechanical parameterization enables a prosthesis control architecture across diverse terrain conditions while preserving key features of AB biomechanics without requiring independently tuned impedance parameter sets for each locomotion mode. To facilitate reproducibility and further development, the biomechanics modeling and impedance parameterization code is publicly available at https://github.com/kim2576.</p></details> | This work has been submitted to a research journal for possible publication. Copyright may be transferred without notice, after which this version may no longer be accessible |
| **[Ab Initio Accuracy Beyond the Cutoff: Distributed Message-Passing Equivariant Attention at Tens-of-Millions-of-Atom Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06122v1)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Molecular dynamics predicts molecular motion from atomic forces and trajectory integration. Density functional theory (DFT) provides first-principles forces, but its $O(N^3)$ cost limits simulations to thousands of atoms; empirical force fields scale to millions but miss bond breaking and charge transfer. Equivariant message-passing Transformers learn the ab initio potential at linear cost. Stacked layers propagate information beyond a single cutoff, giving four layers a receptive field up to 23Å. At this frontier, scale is limited by communication: layers exchange boundary features across devices, force backpropagation repeats the exchange, and moving atoms change neighbor graphs and tensor shapes. We present Tessera, a distributed runtime that fixes workload shapes between rebuilds. It sorts atoms along a space-filling curve into equal-count shards, ensuring identical atom counts and tensor shapes per rank. Peer sets scale as $K^{0.5}$, not $K$. A conservative neighbor skin preserves topology across updates, enabling each rebuild interval to replay as one CUDA Graph. Fused kernels, three-level neighbor search, and overlapped All-to-All complete it. On 512 GPUs, Tessera sustains 30.7M atoms at 89.7% weak-scaling efficiency (77--90% across three per-GPU loads); per-step time rises 1.11--1.29x as machine and problem grow 16x. Graph reuse cuts full-step time $1.87\times$. Across an $8\times$ device increase, neighbor-search cost grows $1.7\times$, versus $3.0\times$ for Morton order. In a live $10.24$M-atom salt solution, NVE energy drift is $0.42\,μ$eV/atom/ps over $10$ ps, and structure matches a serial reference. A 10.06M-atom Norovirus capsid sustains 100 ps of NVT at $\approx 8$ ps/day on 256 A100 GPUs. Over 200,000 steps, it has no broken bonds or anomalously close pairs and uses three compiled variants, with no graph breaks or eager fallbacks.</p></details> |  |
| **[Agentic Behavior Engineering: A Design-by-Contract Methodology for Specifying, Verifying, and Governing Enterprise AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05454v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Enterprise teams that deploy large language model (LLM) agents inherit a maintenance problem that traditional testing does not address. The model is non-deterministic, but the larger risk is that most of what changes an agent's behavior (a retired model version, a revised policy, an upstream schema change, a prompt edit meant to fix one case) originates outside the owning team and is discovered as a production defect rather than caught as a regression. This paper presents Agentic Behavior Engineering (ABE), a platform-agnostic methodology that treats observable agent behavior, rather than the prompt or the model, as the version-controlled engineering artifact. ABE adapts Design by Contract to non-deterministic systems. A specification-only behavior contract with stable rule identifiers states what the agent must do, must not do, and when it must abstain for lack of evidence; a lightweight scenario library turns each rule into concrete situations; and every engineering artifact (tests, runtime policies, telemetry) references the contract in one direction only, so that specification and implementation can change independently without drifting apart. The method organizes the work into five responsibilities (Specify, Ground, Control, Verify, Observe), treats missing evidence and a verified negative as different things, enforces authority outside the model, gates releases on per-scenario pass^k reliability rather than aggregate accuracy, and classifies every production run against the contract so that each finding routes to the asset that must change. We illustrate the method with an executable capacity-allocation boundary suite and a constructed worked example of a product-review approval workflow.</p></details> | 30 pages, 6 figures, 5 tables. Expanded version of the author's practitioner guide "Agentic behavior engineering" in the Microsoft Azure Architecture Center (CC BY 4.0): https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/architecture/ai-ml/guide/agentic-behavior-engineering |

| **[ThermalSplat: Generalizable Pose-Free Gaussian Splatting for Sparse-View Thermal 3D Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15435v1)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Thermal infrared imaging is vital for applications like search-and-rescue and autonomous navigation, yet trans- lating it into 3D scene representations remains a critical bottleneck. The low-texture nature and view-dependent emissivity of thermal images cause standard pose estima- tors to fail catastrophically, collapsing standard 3D Gaus- sian Splatting pipelines under sparse-view constraints. Fur- thermore, existing thermal reconstruction methods rely heavily on dense capture and computationally expensive per-scene optimization. Critically, many of these ap- proaches depend on aligned RGB-thermal datasets to ob- tain reliable camera poses and initial point clouds, ren- dering them unusable in purely thermal, unposed envi- ronments. To break this bottleneck, we present Thermal- Splat, the first generalizable, feed-forward Gaussian splat- ting framework that reconstructs 3D scenes directly from sparse thermal images without requiring known camera poses, RGB guidance, or per-scene training. We over- come thermal-specific challenges using a soft census loss for brightness-invariant structural matching and a bound- ary structure loss to explicitly supervise temperature discon- tinuities. Trained on an aggregated multi-source dataset, ThermalSplat synthesizes sharp reconstructions of unseen scenes in a single feed-forward pass. Extensive experi- ments demonstrate that ThermalSplat holds a strict advan- tage over scene-specific thermal splatting methods in the sparse regime. Furthermore, even compared against these baselines operating under their ideal dense capture set- tings, ThermalSplat achieves superior perceptual fidelity. By eliminating the need for computationally expensive per scene optimization, ThermalSplat offers an efficient and generalizable approach to thermal 3D reconstruction.</p></details> | WACV 2027 |

| **[A Mode-Unified, Continuous, and Biomimetic Control Strategy of a Robotic Lower Limb Prosthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19183v2)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Existing biomimetic lower-limb prosthesis controllers are commonly designed for discrete locomotion modes, requiring separate parameterizations for level walking, ramps, and stairs. | **[Ab Initio Accuracy Beyond the Cutoff: Distributed Message-Passing Equivariant Attention at Tens-of-Millions-of-Atom Scale](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.06122v1)** | 2026-11-04 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Molecular dynamics predicts molecular motion from atomic forces and trajectory integration. Equivariant message-passing Transformers learn the ab initio potential at linear cost. Stacked layers propagate information beyond a single cutoff, giving four layers a receptive field up to 23Å. We present Tessera, a distributed runtime that fixes workload shapes between rebuilds. It sorts atoms along a space-filling curve into equal-count shards, ensuring identical atom counts and tensor shapes per rank. Peer sets scale as $K^{0.5}$, not $K$. A conservative neighbor skin preserves topology across updates, enabling each rebuild interval to replay as one CUDA Graph. Fused kernels, three-level neighbor search, and overlapped All-to-All complete it. On 512 GPUs, Tessera sustains 30.7M atoms at 89.7% weak-scaling efficiency (77--90% across three per-GPU loads); per-step time rises 1.11--1.29x as machine and problem grow 16x. Graph reuse cuts full-step time $1.87\times$. Across an $8\times$ device increase, neighbor-search cost grows $1.7\times$, versus $3.0\times$ for Morton order. | **[Agentic Behavior Engineering: A Design-by-Contract Methodology for Specifying, Verifying, and Governing Enterprise AI Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05454v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Enterprise teams that deploy large language model (LLM) agents inherit a maintenance problem that traditional testing does not address. ABE adapts Design by Contract to non-deterministic systems. A specification-only behavior contract with stable rule identifiers states what the agent must do, must not do, and when it must abstain for lack of evidence; a lightweight scenario library turns each rule into concrete situations; and every engineering artifact (tests, runtime policies, telemetry) references the contract in one direction only, so that specification and implementation can change independently without drifting apart. The method organizes the work into five responsibilities (Specify, Ground, Control, Verify, Observe), treats missing evidence and a verified negative as different things, enforces authority outside the model, gates releases on per-scenario pass^k reliability rather than aggregate accuracy, and classifies every production run against the contract so that each finding routes to the asset that must change. Expanded version of the author's practitioner guide "Agentic behavior engineering" in the Microsoft Azure Architecture Center (CC BY 4.0): https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/architecture/ai-ml/guide/agentic-behavior-engineering |
| **[Beyond the Bochner Method: Optimal Spectral Gaps for Block Dynamics of Spin Systems like the $O(N)$ Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.05228v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Let $μ$ be a probability distribution on a spin system with $n$ sites and possibly infinite spin spaces. We also obtain spectral bounds for swap walks and local spectral expansion of partite simplicial complexes. For complexes formed from disjoint groups of ranks in random flags of subspaces over $\mathbb F_q$, we obtain a one-sided local spectral expansion bound of $2\sqrt q/(q+1)$. This bound is independent of the number and sizes of each group, and is nontrivial for every prime power $q$. Finally, for random forests $F$ of any finite graph with arbitrary positive edge weights, we prove that \[ \Pr(e,f\in F)\le \frac{4}{3} \Pr(e\in F)\Pr(f\in F) \] for distinct edges $e,f$, improving the factor 2 supplied by general log-concavity bounds for matroid independent sets. The argument combines our extended Bochner method with the analysis of an auxiliary real stable polynomial.</p></details> | 42 pages |

| **[GC-MoE on SM120: Overlapping Expert-Parallel Token Dispatch with Warp-Level FP8xFP4 Block-Scaled MMA on Consumer Blackwell](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.16219v1)** | 2026-11-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Expert-parallel Mixture-of-Experts decoding is latency bound: each layer serializes a token exchange, a grouped GEMM over routed experts, and a combine, while only a few tokens per rank are in flight. Prior GC-MoE fused these phases into one persistent, Green-Context-partitioned CUDA Graph on Hopper (SM90) and datacenter Blackwell (SM100). This paper rebuilds the operator for SM120 (compute capability 12.0, e.g. RTX PRO 5000) over PCIe. SM120 exposes block-scaled tensor cores only through warp-level mma.sync.aligned.kind::mxf8f6f4: no tensor memory, no two-CTA MMA, no cluster multicast. We rebuild the kernel around a swap-AB warp-level block-scaled MMA with FP4 weights in the register A operand and FP8 activations in B, plus SM120-specific mechanisms: scale-byte selection through MMA immediates, a compact-weights path, an adaptive token-tile geometry, an expert-granular streaming dispatch with a batched bulk-pull engine and a 64-bit deduplicating pull cache, full-width BF16 returns drained by resident relay copiers over a position-major arena, and shared experts folded into the compute role. On 8xRTX PRO 5000 over PCIe the operator is 1.522x and 1.387x faster than the FlashInfer CuTe DSL W4A8 MegaMoE and the Cake MegaMoE across 18 decode points (18/18 wins each), and 1.388x faster than Cake with shared experts fused; all 94 numerical cases and 752 per-rank checks pass, at cross-operator cosine similarity of at least 0.9999997.</p></details> | 24 pages, 10 tables, 6 figures |
| **[LILA: Data-Free Structured Pruning of LLMs via Latent Spectral Geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.11163v2)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Structured pruning of large language models (LLMs) has universally required calibration data, gradient computation, or large auxiliary policy networks at pruning time. LILA (\emph{Latent-Informed Layer Analysis}) demonstrates that this dependency is avoidable: each FFN weight matrix is factorized with Non-negative Matrix Factorization (NMF) to obtain a parts-based spectral representation, and neuron importance is scored via the Kolmogorov--Smirnov (KS) distance between singular value distributions of the full and neuron-ablated NMF matrix, a closed-form criterion requiring no calibration corpus, forward pass, or gradient computation. Two data-free matrix variants (Abs, Split) generate pruning masks from weight spectral geometry alone; a calibration-guided variant (Act) is provided as an internal ablation. Evaluation spans 7 model families (up to 70B). Without fine-tuning, data-free LILA surpasses PruneNet (45M-parameter RL policy) by up to 2.12~pp in zero-shot accuracy on LLaMA-2-7B and outperforms WikiText-2-calibrated SliceGPT by up to 6.1~pp across all sparsity levels, confirming data-free performance is not an artifact of omitting activation statistics. After one LoRA RFT epoch (matched WikiText-2 protocol), LILA Act surpasses SliceGPT+RFT by 6.39~pp on average using 98.4\% fewer calibration samples (16 vs.\ 1,024) across LLaMA-2-7B and Phi-2. NTK analysis confirms a $17{\times}$ reduction in functional distortion versus random pruning, providing theoretical grounding for the spectral criterion.</p></details> | 31 Pages |
| **[A Multi-Parent Edge Assembly Crossover Driven Hybrid Genetic Algorithm for the Hamiltonian \textit{p}-Median Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.15219v1)** | 2026-11-11 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The Hamiltonian \textit{p}-median problem seeks to partition the vertices of a graph into exactly $p$ disjoint cycles while minimizing their total cost. This paper presents a hybrid genetic algorithm incorporating a multi-parent edge assembly crossover operator. The crossover combines a base solution with multiple donor solutions in a single recombination operation, thereby expanding the space of available edge exchanges. It uses virtual copies of the base solution to establish edge balance, together with an auxiliary directed graph and a budgeted search to construct valid AB-cycles. Extensive experiments on 215 benchmark instances across four sets show that the proposed algorithm improves the best-known solution values for 34 instances and matches those for another 168. Additional experiments indicate that a moderate number of parents provides beneficial diversity, whereas excessive parent counts, particularly when $p$ is large, lead to excessive population dispersion and hinder convergence.</p></details> |  |
| **[An exact compress-with-another threshold in a binary automaton family](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07044v2)** | 2026-11-10 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>For a state $q$ in a synchronizing automaton, let $μ(q)$ be the minimum length of a word merging $q$ with another initial state. Szykuła asked whether $μ(q)=O(n)$ for strongly connected synchronizing automata and described a binary family attributed to Dżyga, with $n=3p$ states and a conjectured threshold $4n/3$. We specify the boundary transitions omitted from the published piecewise definition and prove that the resulting automata $\mathcal A_p$ satisfy $μ(q_0)=4p=4n/3$ for all $p\geq3$. A shortest word merging $q_0$ with another state is $(ba)^p(ab)^p$. We also construct a reset word of length $3p^2+4p-1$. Consequently, any uniform linear upper bound for $μ(q)$ on binary strongly connected synchronizing automata with corank-one letters has leading constant at least $4/3$.</p></details> | 9 pages, 1 figure. Revised proofs and exposition, corrected boundary transitions, updated references, and added a coauthor |
| **[Ground-Wing: A Parametric 3D Wing-in-Ground-Effect Dataset for Benchmarking Neural Surrogate Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04571v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>This paper presents a CFD dataset of a highly parametrized 3D wing operating in ground effect, designed for benchmarking neural surrogate models for aerodynamic prediction, using a wing shape and flow conditions typically encountered in motorsport applications. The dataset comprises 770 RANS simulations of 194 unique wing configurations, sampled across 27 airfoil profile variants and 4 distances from the ground, spanning variations in wing sweep, chord, taper, angle of attack, and tip twist. Six neural network architectures representing distinct paradigms --- convolutional (DoMINO), graph-based (X-MeshGraphNet), transformer-based (AB-UPT, GeoTransolver, GAOT), and JEPA-based (AeroJEPA) --- are evaluated on their ability to predict surface pressure coefficient and wall shear stress distributions, from which integral drag and lift coefficients are recovered. The dataset is intended to be used as a challenging benchmark for surrogate CFD models, due to the uniqueness of the ground effect being absent from other wing and airfoil datasets. The dataset is available for download at https://huggingface.co/datasets/andreacenzato/Ground-Wing</p></details> |  |
| **[Active Learning for Gaussian Process Regression Under Self-Induced Boltzmann Weights](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.10654v2)** | 2026-11-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We consider the active learning problem where the goal is to learn an unknown function with low prediction error under an unknown Boltzmann distribution induced by the function itself. This self-induced weighting arises naturally in problems such as potential energy surface (PES) modeling in computational chemistry, yet poses unique challenges as the target distribution is unknown and its partition function is intractable. We propose \texttt{AB-SID-iVAR}, a Gaussian Process-based acquisition function that approximates the intractable Bayesian target distribution in closed form while avoiding partition function estimation, and is applicable to both discrete and continuous input domains. We also analyze a Thompson sampling alternative (\texttt{TS-SID-iVAR}) as a higher variance Monte Carlo variant. Despite the unknown target, under mild conditions, we establish that the terminal prediction error vanishes with high probability, and provide a tighter average-case guarantee.
